 Gualicho
 Review: After a hiatus of four years, Costas returned to vinyl earlier this year via the self-released 'It's Me Again' EP. Here he steps up his revival by providing the first ever release on freshly launched label Made For The Dancefloor. The Spanish producer delivers the goods from the word go, peppering a rolling and rubbery house groove with 90s style progressive house sonics and psychedelic acid lines on 'All I Need'. 'Atomic Condition' offers a darker and even more mind-bending spin on the same signature sound, while 'Nothing is Real' is moodier and more sonically warped with nods to 21st century tech-house. Closing cut 'Gualicho', meanwhile, is an ethereal, slow-build delight that delivers laidback electro with a 90s ambient techno twist. … Read more

 If You Still Love Me (7:31)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1168390-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1168390-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Boogalou (6:57)
 Review: Marc Cotterell has been going about his business with great style in recent years. The Plastik People boss leans into the old school soulful house and garage house scenes for his inspiration and updates those foundational samples with plenty of musicality as well as club-ready rhythms. This solo outing kicks off with the impossibly infectious 'Keep Pushing' with its angelic vocal tones and rich piano chords all overflowing with catchy, feel-good vibes. 'If You Still Love Me' has the same irresistibly joyous results, but this time darts about with a touch of US garage swing and some vibrant horns. 'Boogalou' closes with a more knotted, funky bassline. Brilliantly accessible but sophisticated stuff.

 HypnoFreak (8:19)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1181414-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1181414-01-02-01.mp3] 
 All Night Long (7:43)
 Review: Relaunched turn-of-the-Millennium West Coast house imprint Pacific Coast Recordings continues to serve up must-check reissues. This two-tracker first appeared as a promo-only missive on the label's long dormant Pacific Traxx offshoot and comes from peripheral crew member (and regular JT Donaldson collaborator) Chris Penny AKA CPEN. 'Hypno Freak' paddles in the funkier end of the California deep house spectrum, with crisp funk guitar licks, tight electric piano stabs and gritty spoken word vocals rising above a weighty bassline and heavy, locked-in beats. In contrast, 'All Night Long' is a more traditional chunk of lightly tech-tinged peak-time San Francisco deep house with just the right amount of frazzled late-night funk. … Read more

 F#UK The World To The Core (7:32)
 Review: Swiss producer Dan Piu is a true techno veteran, with an expansive catalogue that reaches back to the early '90s. His return to form in recent years has been a delight, with classic tracks and new productions alike resurfacing for a new generation of heads to fawn over. Following recent drops on Childhood Intelligence, Ovnie and Common Dreams, Piu rocks up to Hizou Deep Rooted Music with some stellar machine musings. "Quantum Computing" is a warm and soulful house groover with rich layers of synth work at its core, while "Robots In Rage" takes a more angular approach with skittering techno rhythms and plenty of analogue synth wriggles. "A Machine Will Never Do X" is pristine electro, and "F#UK The World To The Core" completes the set with a sumptuous ambient techno trip oozing sophistication and vintage atmospheres in equal measure. … Read more

 Ricardo Angeles - "Kids Playing" (6:26)
 Review: For their seventh vinyl releases, Peaces - the Russian imprint previously known as Peace Symphonies - has offered up a split EP featuring tracks from two of its numerous affiliated artists. Daniel handles side A, pairing the softly spun, gently sunny and shuffling deep house positivity of 'From Here To There in Under a Second' with the deliciously dreamy, piano-splashed, breakdown-rich deep tech-house beauty of 'The World is Full of Light'. Ricardo Angeles steps up on side B, first unveiling the deep, dreamy progressive house retro-futurism of 'Changes', before doffing a cap to the immersive, dancefloor-ready sound worlds of the popular All Day I Dream label. … Read more

 Mixed Emotions (Trinidian Deep remix) (8:31)
 Review: Glenn Davis is a Dublin DJ with 25 years behind the decks and a few cool records with Ashley Beedle to his name, alongside productions for Deeper Groove, Yore Records, Ascension On Wax, Mate Records, Selections, Silver Walker, Ele Records and various remixes for Wolf, Local Talk, Adeen and more. In 2012, he began making his own deep house, and now he co-runs Forma with Keith Dalton. The second release on the label is a rolling deep-house original, all driving bassline, jazzy chords and synth stabs hanging in a hypnotic haze. A perfectly warm vibe for cosy dance floors. Flip it over for Trinidadian Deep, whose signature Caribbean flavour adds live percussion, organs and a sub-bass that wants a proper system to really show it at its best. A very tidy 12". … Read more

 Jump & Prance (club mix) (5:28)

 I Can Feel It (club mix) (6:53)
 Review: Cabaret Records revisits a stone cold NYC deep house classic from 1990 - a rare release as Dreamhouse from man of many aliases Joey Longo, best known as Pal Joey. Lusciously loved-up, gently melodious and lightly jazzy, the A-side 'Dream Mix' of 'Jump & Prance' is particularly potent, sitting somewhere between the warming headiness of contemporaneous Burrell brothers releases and the sun-soaked colour of Italian dream house. Turn to side B for the more warehouse-ready, dub-fused deep house excellence of the 'Club Mix', featuring verses from dancehall toaster Ceasar, and the 'Club Mix' of the Samson & Delilah-sampling 'I Can Feel It'. Simply essential. … Read more

 Soar

 Soar (Ivan Solo dub)
 Review: For the latest missive from his essential (and expansive) Elements of Life combo, Masters at Work man Louie Vega has recruited singer Lisa Fischer, a long-serving soulstress best known for her work with Luther Vandross and a string of fine solo records in the early 1990s. 'Soar' is predictably gorgeous and sun-soaked, with Fischer's sublime lead vocals riding waves of Latin percussion, jazzy guitar licks, shuffling house beats and sustained piano motifs. It comes accompanied by Vega's own 'Ivan Solo Dub', where bittersweet horn solos rise above a thicker and more densely layered take on the original groove. It's a fine alternative mix that's every bit as essential as the full vocal A-side version. … Read more

 Waiting Now (Without Chords) (4:54)
 Review: Earlier this year, Turkish artist Zeynep Erbay returned to action after a couple of years away, in the process following up admired releases on NuNorthern Soul and Soul Clap Records with the inaugural release on the Moments On The Floor label. Here she returns to that imprint with a quartet of nostalgic, retro-futurist house jams. Opener 'Dans' sees her wrap a late 80s style house groove in dreamy chords, MK style organ licks and deliciously wild and psychedelic acid lines, while 'Find The Girl' is a rushing and Kaleidoscopic slab of boat party ready piano house sunshine. Over on side B Erbay drops two takes on Phuture style acid jacker 'Waiting Now - one embellished with gorgeously loved-up chords and the other without. … Read more

 Smooth Architecture (5:22)
 Review: Smooth Architecture is in many ways an odd title, but a fitting one. Kyle Hall has long been Detroit's house golden boy and though the music he makes is infused with aching soul and seductive pads, his drums are pretty much always roughed up, dusty, and frayed around the edges. That is true again of all four cuts are, starting with 'Venus Deep' with its cosmic melodies circling around rugged analogue drums. 'Velour Focus' (83 West remix) has a shuffle and skip to it that is more late-night, and the sombre chords reflect that, while the original version is deeper and more pensive. 'Smooth Architecture' then bumps along nicely with twitchy acid stabs and plenty of analogue hiss. More timeless gold from Hall. … Read more

 Keisha (4:13)
 Review: Detroit house architect Kyle Hall never seems to be trying too hard, does he? His tunes always sound like he just flicked a switch and let the machines do their thing. That's kind of the magic of 'MJ FLP' with its breezy drums, pads that swoop in and out and a female vocal dropped somewhere in the mix that just hits right. 'Guyanese Deep' pulls back a bit, drum loops that feel very Seven Davis Jr., and with vocals buried lower this time making it more of a slow-burn thing. Then, closing out this reissue of an EP that first dropped at the start of the year is 'Ash FLP' which is broken and loose with proper r&b warmth running through it, and 'Keisha' with soft golden chords that feel made for 3 am.

 Don't Stop (6:08)
 Review: Ossie grew up in East London on r&b, garage and grime, started making beats at twelve with The Neptunes as his heroes and still has a sound defined by those facts all these years on. After a big EP on Dam Swindle's Heist already, he follows it up with more seductively deep but irresistibly dancey and well-swung genre-fusions. This four-tracker opens with Miraa May scatting through the title cut, and then comes a loopy piano house number packed with feel-good joy but never cheap. A sampled "I believe... oh yes I do" groover follows to keep the smiles wide then the finish comes as a cavernous warehouse stomper for when the lights turn a little lower and the energy gets more wild. … Read more

 The World Goes Oriental (7:03)
 Review: Since debuting in the mid-1990s, Kurt Spichiger aka Shaka has released rather a lot of high-quality deep house, in the process notching up appearances on the likes of Local Talk, Traxx Underground, Yore, Housewax and, most recently, Mate. Here he evokes the atmosphere of a 'smoky' basement club via a three-track Seasons Limited label debut. Title track 'Smoky Club' is undeniably classy and carefully crafted, with starry electronic motifs, dreamy pads and jammed-out Wurlitzer organ motifs rising above a languid, leisurely deep house groove. Spichiger's love of jazz comes to the fore on the even warmer and more seductive 'City Park' - all sampled disco drums, smooth jazz-funk bass and extended electric piano solos - while 'The World Goes Oriental' sounds like vintage Larry Heard mixed with the afterglow of late night lovin'. … Read more

 Review: French label Phonogramme continues its productive partnership with long-serving Toronto producer Austin Bascom aka Abacus - one that has already brought us a wealth of reissues and previously unheard material. The Earthly Pleasures EP initially landed on 83 West Records way back in 1996 and here returns in remastered form. As it did 30 years ago, the three-tracker begins with 'Sunrayz', a gorgeous chunk of enveloping, rhythmically infectious deep house laden with dreamy pads, elastic jazz-funk bass, held organ chords and wriggling TB-303 acid lines.  Baobab Bonanza
 Review: Studio Barnhus regulars Ari Bald and CJ Scott never fail to impress, as this fourth EP for the popular Swedish imprint proves. They go for the jugular immediately on carnival-ready opener 'Trippin Totems', a fiendishly percussive, Batacuda-inspired house jam showcasing their love of Brazilian musical culture, before blending Latin jazz motifs, sparkling piano riffs and more sweat-soaked house Afro-Latin drums on the similarly excellent 'Coral Capers'. The Swedish twosome nudge closer to tribal techno on the breathless and otherworldly 'Mole Patrol', while 'Baobab Bonanza' sits somewhere between jazz-house, samba-house and something altogether deeper and more dub-fired. If you love drums, you need this in your life. … Read more

 Pharoah's Message (WATH Blowing East radio edit)
 Review: Earlier in the year, Right Tempo dropped an EP packed with extremely good Joaquin 'Joe' Clausell reconstructions of Italian saxophonist Emmanuele Cisi's 'Pharoah's Message', a 21st century spiritual jazz workout that pays tribute to the late, great Pharoah Sanders. Here they serve up 'phase two' of the project via a four-tracker packed with previously unheard takes. Clausell returns to kick things off with an alternative pass on his warming, sun-soaked and percussion-packed 'Sacred Rhythm' version, before Didje Doo delivers vocal and instrumental interpretations that join the dots between neo-classical and experimental ambient. Finally, WATH Bowling East lays down a radio version that presents the track as a gorgeous jazz-house gem. … Read more

 All The Same (2:44)
 Review: Three years ago, British-Nigerian poet, producer and beat-maker Joshua Idehen made his full-length debut with Learn To Swim: A Mixtape, a gloriously atmospheric, soulful and musically rich affair that quickly sold out its run of 300 copies. Thankfully, there are far more copies around of this Heavenly Recordings reissue. It remains a glorious set, with Idehen and his various musical collaborators blending dusty, organic and jazz-flecked deep house with nods to soul, jazz, drum and bass, post-dubstep beats, ambient and forthright, warehouse-ready workouts. Throughout, Idehen's beautiful spoken word poetry and rhymes sit atop, drawing the listener in. It's a genuine modern classic that deserves wider attention. … Read more

 Distant Light
 Review: Amour, Jamie 'Jimpster' Odell's fourth studio album, should be considered a key staging post in the long-serving Essex artist's career. It captures the moment when the Freerange co-founder began to embrace deep house after years exploring downtempo and nu-jazz sounds. Here reissued on vinyl to mark the set's 20th birthday, it remains a lovingly crafted and superbly realised set. There are atmospheric and immersive low-tempo numbers, of course - see the jazzy and drowsy head-nod 'In An Analogue Way', the slo-mo jazz-house-soul of 'Slippin' and the warming nu-jazz lusciousness of 'Secret Sun' - but also oodles of club-ready workouts, with highlights including mutant hip-house gem 'Left to Right' (featuring rapper Capitol A), the ultra-deep shuffle of 'Seventh Wave' and the gorgeously immersive and emotive loveliness of 'I Want It'. … Read more

 On Tick
 Review: Melbourne bassist Henry Hicks goes by the name Horatio Luna when he's not pocket thumping with East Coast Collective, 30/70 or Tenderlonious, among many other collaborations on his bulging, seam-testing CV. Now for his own time in the lime: Ciccin was originally self-released digitally in January 2026, but its support by DJs and broadcasters has now justified its latest vinyl rendition here. Backed by a close-knit and attentive band, 'Bell Crash' has a high-quality, virtuosic opening improv before ascending into frenetic bop, while club-side stompers like 'New Don' bring desert modular ambience to funk beats so swung they're yucky. … Read more

 Review: Defected Records' fourth classics volume is upon us; again, the label gather 12 of their most established and sought-after releases from across the catalogue. First included is Roger Sanchez's 'Another Chance', the label's first UK number one, followed by choice, deepest highlights: Dennis Ferrer's remix of Fish Go Deep's 'The Cure & The Cause', then on the B, Blaze Presents UDA's 'Most Precious Love' and Kings Of Tomorrow's 'Fall For You.'  Bralan Arias - "Sisifo"
 Review: Spain's Mate reaches out a comforting hand in musical form with a second volume of their Music Therapy series. It's a rework that opens up and finds Anthonius's 'Let Me Love U' taking on a soul-drenched Afro vibe with a heartfelt vocal and big but soft bass that really cocoons you. Laroye's 'The Spirit Of House' is smooth as Galaxy chocolate and decorated with deft cosmic touches then Rafa Santos brings a percussive broken-beat skip and Latin heat to 'Bossa Seeds'. Bralan Arias leans into splashy jazz and life-affirming melodies on the elegant 'Sisifo'. Super classy. … Read more

 Untitled 26
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1188964-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1188964-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Long Stories
 Review: Deep house lovers will always have their head turned by a new slab from US maestro DJ Aakmael. His trademark sampling is back to woo and charm across three more whimsical tunes that will appeal to lovers of early Smallville releases. 'Off Grid' is light, airy and uplifting in a quiet and introspective manner, then 'Untitled 26' stretches its legs a bit with a more roomy and expansive deep house jaunt through cosmic chord work and classic FM baselines. 'Long Stories' then smudges and smears synths over a loose percussive jumble. It's lush house music with aching heart and soul as ever from Aakmael.
 … Read more

 This website uses cookies We (Juno Media Ltd) use cookies to personalise content and ads, to provide social media features and to analyse our traffic.  Strictly Necessary
 These cookies are required to deliver the service or functionality you request. They enable core features such as page navigation, secure access, authentication, and load balancing. Without them, the website cannot function properly. Consent is not required for these cookies.
 Performance
 These cookies collect information about how visitors use our website, such as page visits and traffic sources. This data helps us measure performance and improve our site and services. These cookies are only used with your consent.
 Affiliate and Marketing
 These cookies are set by our advertising partners to track browsing activity and build profiles based on your interests. This allows personalised advertising and remarketing on other websites. These cookies are only used with your consent.
